| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | I am an humble suitor to your virtues;<br>For pity is the virtue of the law,<br>And none but tyrants use it cruelly.<br>It pleases [[motifs/time\|time]] and fortune to lie heavy<br>Upon a friend of mine, who in hot [[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>Hath stepped into the law, which is past depth<br>To those that without heed do plunge into’t.<br>He is a man, setting his feat aside,<br>Of comely virtues;<br>Nor did he soil the fact with cowardice—<br>An [[concepts/honour\|honour]] in him which buys out his fault—<br>But with a noble fury and fair spirit,<br>Seeing his reputation touched to [[motifs/death\|death]],<br>He did oppose his foe;<br>And with such sober and unnoted passion<br>He did behave his anger, ere ’twas spent,<br>As if he had but proved an argument. |
| FIRST SENATOR | You undergo too strict a [[concepts/paradox\|paradox]],<br>Striving to make an ugly deed look fair.<br>Your words have took such pains as if they laboured<br>To bring manslaughter into form, and set quarrelling<br>Upon the head of valour—which indeed<br>Is valour misbegot, and came into the world<br>When sects and factions were newly born.<br>He’s truly valiant that can wisely suffer<br>The worst that man can breathe, and make his<br>wrongs his outsides<br>To wear them like his raiment carelessly,<br>And ne‘er prefer his injuries to his heart<br>To bring it into danger.<br>If wrongs be evils and enforce us kill,<br>What folly ’tis to hazard life for ill! |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | My lord— |
| FIRST SENATOR | You cannot make gross sins look clear.<br>To [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]] is no valour, but to bear. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | My lords, then, under favour, pardon me<br>If I speak like a captain.<br>Why do fond men expose themselves to battle,<br>And not endure all threats, [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] upon‘t,<br>And let the foes quietly cut their throats<br>Without repugnancy? If there be<br>Such valour in the bearing, what make we<br>Abroad? Why then, women are more valiant<br>That stay at [[motifs/home\|home]] if bearing carry it,<br>And the ass more captain than the [[motifs/lion\|lion]], the felon<br>Loaden with irons wiser than the judge,<br>If wisdom be in [[concepts/suffering\|suffering]]. O my lords,<br>As you are great, be pitifully good.<br>Who cannot condemn rashness in cold [[motifs/blood\|blood]]?<br>To kill, I grant, is sin’s extremest gust,<br>But in defence, by [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]], ’tis most just.<br>To be in anger is impiety,<br>But who is man that is not angry?<br>Weigh but the crime with this. |
| SECOND SENATOR | You breathe in vain. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | In vain?<br>His service done at Lacedaemon and Byzantium<br>Were a sufficient briber for his life. |
| FIRST SENATOR | What’s that? |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Why, I say, my lords, he’s done fair service,<br>And slain in fight many of your enemies.<br>How full of valour did he bear himself<br>In the last conflict, and made plenteous wounds! |
| SECOND SENATOR | He has made too much plenty with ‘em.<br>He’s a sworn rioter; he has a sin<br>That often drowns him and takes his valour prisoner.<br>If there were no foes, that were enough<br>To overcome him. In that beastly fury<br>He has been known to commit outrages<br>And cherish factions. ’Tis inferred to us<br>His days are foul and his drink dangerous. |
| FIRST SENATOR | He dies. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Hard fate! He might have died in war.<br>My lords, if not for any parts in him—<br>Though his right arm might purchase his own [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>And be in debt to none—yet more to move you,<br>Take my deserts to his and join ’em both.<br>And for I know<br>Your reverend ages [[motifs/love\|love]] security,<br>I’ll pawn my victories, all my [[concepts/honour\|honour]] to you<br>Upon his good returns.<br>If by this crime he owes the law his life,<br>Why, let the war receive’t in valiant gore,<br>For law is strict, and war is nothing more. |
| FIRST SENATOR | We are for law; he dies. Urge it no more,<br>On height of our displeasure. Friend or brother,<br>He forfeits his own [[motifs/blood\|blood]] that spills another. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Must it be so? It must not be.<br>My lords, I do beseech you know me. |
| SECOND SENATOR | How? |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Call me to your remembrances. |
| THIRD SENATOR | What? |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | I cannot think but your age has forgot me.<br>It could not else be I should prove so base<br>To sue and be denied such common grace.<br>My wounds ache at you. |
| FIRST SENATOR | Do you dare our anger?<br>’Tis in few words, but spacious in effect:<br>We banish thee for ever. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Banish me?<br>Banish your dotage, banish usury<br>That makes the senate ugly. |
| FIRST SENATOR | If after two days’ shine<br>Athens contain thee, attend our weightier judgement;<br>And, not to swell your spirit, he shall be<br>Executed presently. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Senators and attendants |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Now the gods keep you old enough that you may live<br>Only in bone, that none may look on you!<br>I’m worse than mad. I have kept back their foes<br>While they have told their money and let out<br>Their coin upon large interest—I myself,<br>Rich only in large hurts. All those for this?<br>Is this the balsam that the usuring senate<br>Pours into captains’ wounds? Banishment!<br>It comes not ill; I hate not to be banished.<br>It is a cause worthy my spleen and fury,<br>That I may strike at Athens. I’ll cheer up<br>My discontented troops, and lay for hearts.<br>’Tis [[concepts/honour\|honour]] with most lands to be at odds.<br>Soldiers should brook as little wrongs as gods. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano tre Senatori da una porta |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Miei signori, avete il mio voto. La colpa è sanguinosa.<br>È necessario che muoia.<br>Nulla incoraggia il peccato quanto la [[concepts/mercy\|clemenza]]. |
| SECONDO SENATORE | Verissimo; la legge lo schiaccerà. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiade]] da un’altra porta, con seguito |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | [[concepts/honour\|Onore]], salute e compassione al senato! |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Ebbene, capitano. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Sono un umile supplice delle vostre virtù;<br>ché la pietà è la virtù della legge,<br>e nessuno se non i tiranni l’usa crudelmente.<br>Piace al [[motifs/time\|tempo]] e alla fortuna di gravare pesantemente<br>sopra un mio amico, il quale in caldo [[motifs/blood\|sangue]]<br>è incorso nella legge, che è di profondità smisurata<br>per quelli che senza cautela vi si tuffano.<br>Egli è uomo, messo da parte il suo atto,<br>di belle virtù;<br>né macchiò il fatto di viltà—<br>un [[concepts/honour\|onore]] in lui che riscatta la sua colpa—<br>ma con nobile furore e franco spirito,<br>vedendo la propria reputazione ferita a [[motifs/death\|morte]],<br>affrontò il suo nemico;<br>e con passione così pacata e composta<br>governò la sua collera, prima che si sfogasse,<br>come se non avesse fatto che sostenere un ragionamento. |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Sostenete un troppo arduo [[concepts/paradox\|paradosso]],<br>sforzandovi di far apparire bella una brutta azione.<br>Le vostre parole si son date tanta pena come se s’affaticassero<br>a mettere in forma l’omicidio, e a porre la rissa<br>sul capo del valore—il quale in verità<br>è valore mal generato, e venne al mondo<br>quando sette e fazioni erano appena nate.<br>Veramente valoroso è colui che sa saggiamente sopportare<br>il peggio che uomo possa proferire, e fare dei propri<br>torti la sua veste esteriore,<br>per portarli con noncuranza come un abito,<br>e mai accostare le offese al cuore<br>per metterlo in pericolo.<br>Se i torti sono mali e ci costringono a uccidere,<br>che follia è rischiare la vita per il male! |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Mio signore— |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Non potete far apparire limpidi i peccati grossolani.<br>[[concepts/revenge\|Vendicarsi]] non è valore, ma sopportare. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Miei signori, allora, con vostra licenza, perdonatemi<br>se parlo da capitano.<br>Perché gli uomini insensati s’espongono alla battaglia,<br>e non sopportano ogni minaccia, e non ci [[motifs/sleep\|dormono]] sopra,<br>e non lasciano che i nemici tranquillamente taglino loro la gola<br>senza opporsi? Se c’è<br>tanto valore nel sopportare, che facciamo noi<br>in campo? Ebbene allora, più valorose sono le donne<br>che restano a [[motifs/home\|casa]], se il sopportare è ciò che conta,<br>e l’asino più capitano del [[motifs/lion\|leone]], e il malfattore<br>carico di ferri più savio del giudice,<br>se la saggezza sta nel [[concepts/suffering\|patire]]. O miei signori,<br>come siete grandi, siate pietosamente buoni.<br>Chi non sa condannare l’avventatezza a [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] freddo?<br>Uccidere, lo concedo, è la raffica estrema del peccato,<br>ma in difesa, per [[concepts/mercy\|misericordia]], è cosa giustissima.<br>Essere in collera è empietà,<br>ma chi è uomo che non s’adiri?<br>Pesate il delitto soltanto con questo. |
| SECONDO SENATORE | Sprecate fiato. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Sprecato?<br>I servigi da lui resi a Lacedemone e a Bisanzio<br>basterebbero a riscattare la sua vita. |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Che c’entra questo? |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Dico, miei signori, ch’egli ha reso bei servigi,<br>e ucciso in battaglia molti dei vostri nemici.<br>Con quanto valore si comportò<br>nell’ultimo scontro, e quante ferite inflisse! |
| SECONDO SENATORE | Ne ha fatta anche troppa abbondanza.<br>È uno sfrenato giurato; ha un vizio<br>che spesso lo annega e gli fa prigioniero il valore.<br>Se non ci fossero nemici, quello basterebbe<br>a vincerlo. In quel furore bestiale<br>è noto che ha commesso oltraggi<br>e alimentato fazioni. Ci vien riferito<br>che i suoi giorni sono turpi e il suo bere pericoloso. |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Muoia. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Dura sorte! Poteva morire in guerra.<br>Miei signori, se non per alcuna sua qualità—<br>benché il suo braccio destro potrebbe comprarsi il proprio [[motifs/time\|tempo]]<br>e non esser debitore a nessuno—pure, per commuovervi di più,<br>prendete i miei meriti insieme ai suoi e uniteli entrambi.<br>E poiché so<br>che le vostre venerande età [[motifs/love\|amano]] la garanzia,<br>vi darò in pegno le mie vittorie, tutto il mio [[concepts/honour\|onore]]<br>sul suo buon ravvedimento.<br>Se per questo delitto egli deve la vita alla legge,<br>ebbene, la riceva la guerra in valoroso sangue,<br>ché la legge è severa, e la guerra non è nulla di più. |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Noi stiamo per la legge; egli muore. Non insistete oltre,<br>a costo del colmo del nostro sdegno. Amico o fratello,<br>perde il proprio [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] chi versa quello d’un altro. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Dev’essere così? Non dev’essere.<br>Miei signori, vi supplico, riconoscetemi. |
| SECONDO SENATORE | Come? |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Richiamatemi alla vostra memoria. |
| TERZO SENATORE | Che cosa? |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Non posso pensare altro se non che la vostra età m’abbia dimenticato.<br>Non potrebbe altrimenti essere ch’io mi mostrassi tanto vile<br>da chiedere e vedermi negare una grazia così comune.<br>Le mie ferite mi dolgono per causa vostra. |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Osate sfidare la nostra collera?<br>È in poche parole, ma ampia negli effetti:<br>ti bandiamo per sempre. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Bandire me?<br>Bandite il vostro rimbambimento, bandite l’usura<br>che rende turpe il senato. |
| PRIMO SENATORE | Se dopo due giorni di sole<br>Atene ti conterrà ancora, aspettati un giudizio più grave;<br>e, per non gonfiare il vostro spirito, egli sarà<br>giustiziato immediatamente. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono i Senatori e il seguito |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Ora gli dèi vi conservino tanto vecchi che possiate vivere<br>solo in ossa, sì che nessuno voglia guardarvi!<br>Sono peggio che pazzo. Io ho tenuto lontani i loro nemici<br>mentre essi contavano il loro denaro e prestavano<br>la loro moneta a grossi interessi—io stesso,<br>ricco soltanto di larghe ferite. Tutto questo per questo?<br>È questo il balsamo che il senato usuraio<br>versa nelle ferite dei capitani? Bando!<br>Non giunge male; non mi spiace essere bandito.<br>È una causa degna del mio livore e del mio furore,<br>ch’io possa colpire Atene. Rincuorerò<br>le mie truppe scontente, e mi metterò a conquistare cuori.<br>È [[concepts/honour\|onore]] essere in urto con la maggior parte dei paesi.<br>I soldati dovrebbero tollerare i torti quanto poco li tollerano gli dèi. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_3--scene_7" data-title="Timon of Athens — Act III, Scene 7" data-chapter="Scene 7" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/villain,motif/birds,motif/city,motif/jewel,motif/leaves,motif/summer,motif/swallow,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,motif/winter,concept/doubt,concept/hope,character/alcibiades,character/lucius,character/lucullus,character/sempronius,character/timon"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter divers of [[characters/timon\|Timon]]’s friends, amongst them [[characters/lucullus\|Lucullus]], [[characters/lucius\|Lucius]], [[characters/sempronius\|Sempronius]], and other Lords and Senators, at several doors |
| FIRST LORD | The good [[motifs/time\|time]] of day to you, sir. |
| SECOND LORD | I also wish it to you. I think this honourable lord did but try us this other day. |
| FIRST LORD | Upon that were my thoughts tiring when we encountered. I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] it is not so low with him as he made it seem in the trial of his several friends. |
| SECOND LORD | It should not be, by the [[Persuasion\|persuasion]] of his new feasting. |
| FIRST LORD | I should think so. He hath sent me an earnest inviting, which many my near occasions did urge me to put off, but he hath conjured me beyond them, and I must needs appear. |
| SECOND LORD | In like manner was I in debt to my importunate business, but he would not hear my excuse. I am sorry when he sent to borrow of me that my provision was out. |
| FIRST LORD | I am sick of that grief too, as I understand how all things go. |
| SECOND LORD | Every man hears so. What would he have borrowed of you? |
| FIRST LORD | A thousand pieces. |
| SECOND LORD | A thousand pieces? |
| FIRST LORD | What of you? |
| SECOND LORD | He sent to me, sir— |
| *(didascalia)* | Loud music. Enter [[characters/timon\|Timon]] and attendants |
| SECOND LORD | Here he comes. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | With all my heart, gentlemen both; and how fare you? |
| FIRST LORD | Ever at the best, hearing [[motifs/well\|well]] of your lordship. |
| SECOND LORD | The [[motifs/swallow\|swallow]] follows not [[motifs/summer\|summer]] more willing than we your lordship. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | (aside) Nor more willingly [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] [[motifs/winter\|winter]], such [[motifs/summer\|summer]] [[motifs/birds\|birds]] are men.—Genttemen, our dinner will not recompense this long stay. Feast your ears with the music a while, if they will fare so harshly o’th’ trumpets’ sound; we shall to’t presently. |
| FIRST LORD | I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] it remains not unkindly with your lordship that I returned you an empty messenger. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | O sir, let it not trouble you. |
| SECOND LORD | My noble lord— |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Ah, my good friend, what cheer? |
| *(didascalia)* | A table and stools are brought in |
| SECOND LORD | My most honourable lord, I am e’en sick of shame that when your lordship this other day sent to me I was so unfortunate a beggar. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Think not on’t, sir. |
| SECOND LORD | If you had sent but two hours before— |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Let it not cumber your better remembrance.—Come, bring in all together. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Servants with covered dishes |
| SECOND LORD | All covered dishes. |
| FIRST LORD | Royal cheer, I warrant you. |
| THIRD LORD | [[concepts/doubt\|Doubt]] not that, if money and the season can yield it. |
| FIRST LORD | How do you? What’s the news? |
| THIRD LORD | [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiades]] is banished. Hear you of it? |
| FIRST | and SECOND LORDS [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiades]] banished? |
| THIRD LORD | ’Tis so, be sure of it. |
| FIRST LORD | How, how? |
| SECOND LORD | I pray you, upon what? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | My worthy friends, will you draw near? |
| THIRD LORD | I’ll tell you more anon. Here’s a noble feast toward. |
| SECOND LORD | This is the old man still. |
| THIRD LORD | Will’t hold, will’t hold? |
| SECOND LORD | It does; but [[motifs/time\|time]] will—and so— |
| THIRD LORD | I do conceive. 64 |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Each man to his stool with that spur as he would to the lip of his mistress. Your diet shall be in all places alike. Make not a [[motifs/city\|city]] feast of it, to let the meat cool ere we can agree upon the first place. Sit, sit. The gods require our thanks.<br>They sit<br>You great benefactors, sprinkle our society with thankfulness. For your own gifts make yourselves praised; but reserve still to give, lest your deities be despised. Lend to each man enough that one need not lend to another; for were your godheads to borrow of men, men would forsake the gods. Make the meat be beloved more than the man that gives it. Let no assembly of twenty be without a score of villains. If there sit twelve women at the table, let a dozen of them be as they are. The rest of your foes, O gods—the senators of Athens, together with the common tag of people—what is amiss in them, you gods, make suitable for destruction. For these my present friends, as they are to me nothing, so in nothing bless them; and to nothing are they welcome.—Uncover, [[dogs]], and lap. |
| *(didascalia)* | The dishes are uncovered, and seen to be full of steaming [[motifs/water\|water]] and stones |
| SOME LORDS | What does his lordship mean? |
| OTHER LORDS | I know not. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | May you a better feast never behold,<br>You knot of mouth-friends. Smoke and lukewarm [[motifs/water\|water]]<br>Is your perfection. This is [[characters/timon\|Timon]]’s last,<br>Who, stuck and spangled with your flattery,<br>Washes it off, and sprinkles in your faces<br>Your reeking villainy. |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws [[motifs/water\|water]] in their faces |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Live loathed and long,<br>Most smiling, smooth, detested parasites,<br>Courteous destroyers, affable wolves, meek bears,<br>You fools of fortune, trencher-friends, [[motifs/time\|time]]’s flies,<br>Cap-and-knee slaves, vapours, and minute-jacks!<br>Of man and beast the infinite malady<br>Crust you quite o’er. |
| *(didascalia)* | A Lord is going |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | What, dost thou go?<br>Soft, take thy physic first. Thou too, and thou. |
| *(didascalia)* | He beats them |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Stay, I will lend thee money, borrow none. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lords, leaving caps and gowns |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | What, all in motion? Henceforth be no feast<br>Whereat a [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]’s not a welcome guest.<br>Burn [[house]]! Sink Athens! Henceforth hated be<br>Of [[characters/timon\|Timon]] man and all humanity! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Senators and other Lords |
| FIRST LORD | How now, my lords? |
| SECOND LORD | Know you the quality of Lord [[characters/timon\|Timon]]’s fury? |
| THIRD LORD | Push! Did you see my cap? |
| FOURTH LORD | I have lost my gown. |
| FIRST LORD | He’s but a mad lord, and naught but humours sways him. He gave me a [[motifs/jewel\|jewel]] th’other day, and now he has beat it out of my hat. Did you see my [[motifs/jewel\|jewel]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | THIRD LORD Did you see my cap? |
| *(didascalia)* | SECOND LORD |
| FIRST LORD | Here ’tis. |
| FOURTH LORD | Here lies my gown. |
| FIRST LORD | Let’s make no stay. |
| SECOND LORD | Lord [[characters/timon\|Timon]]’s mad. |
| THIRD LORD | I feel’t upon my bones. |
| FOURTH LORD | One day he gives us diamonds, next day stones. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano diversi amici di [[characters/timon\|Timone]], fra loro [[characters/lucullus\|Lucullo]], [[characters/lucius\|Lucio]], [[characters/sempronius\|Sempronio]], e altri Signori e Senatori, da diverse porte |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Il buon [[motifs/time\|giorno]] a voi, signore. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Ve lo auguro anch’io. Credo che quest’onorevole signore l’altro giorno non facesse che metterci alla prova. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Su questo si affaticavano i miei pensieri quando c’incontrammo. [[concepts/hope\|Spero]] che non sia in così basso stato come volle far apparire nella prova dei suoi vari amici. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Non dovrebbe esserlo, a [[Persuasion\|persuasione]] del suo nuovo banchetto. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Credo di sì. M’ha mandato un caldo invito, che molte mie prossime occupazioni mi spingevano a declinare, ma egli m’ha scongiurato al di là di esse, e devo pur comparire. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Allo stesso modo ero io debitore ai miei affari pressanti, ma non volle udire la mia scusa. Mi dispiace che, quando mandò a chiedermi in prestito, la mia provvista fosse esaurita. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Anch’io soffro di quel dolore, ora che comprendo come vanno le cose. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Ognuno ne sente parlare. Quanto avrebbe voluto in prestito da voi? |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Mille pezzi. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Mille pezzi? |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | E da voi? |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Mandò da me, signore— |
| *(didascalia)* | Musica alta. Entrano [[characters/timon\|Timone]] e il seguito |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Eccolo che viene. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Con tutto il cuore, gentiluomini entrambi; e come state? |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Sempre al meglio, udendo [[motifs/well\|bene]] di vostra signoria. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | La [[motifs/swallow\|rondine]] non segue l’[[motifs/summer\|estate]] più volentieri di quanto noi seguiamo vostra signoria. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | (a parte) Né più volentieri [[motifs/leaves\|lascia]] l’[[motifs/winter\|inverno]]: tali [[motifs/birds\|uccelli]] d’[[motifs/summer\|estate]] sono gli uomini.—Gentiluomini, il nostro pranzo non ricompenserà questa lunga attesa. Banchettate un poco le vostre orecchie con la musica, se vorranno cibarsi così aspramente del suono delle trombe; ci metteremo a tavola fra poco. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | [[concepts/hope\|Spero]] che vostra signoria non serbi rancore perché vi rimandai un messaggero a mani vuote. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Oh signore, non ve ne date pensiero. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Mio nobile signore— |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Ah, mio buon amico, come va? |
| *(didascalia)* | Vengono portati una tavola e degli sgabelli |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Mio onoratissimo signore, sono proprio malato di vergogna che, quando vostra signoria mandò da me l’altro giorno, io fossi un mendicante così sfortunato. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Non pensateci, signore. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Se solo aveste mandato due ore prima— |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Non ingombri la vostra migliore memoria.—Su, portate tutto insieme. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano i Servi con piatti coperti |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Tutti piatti coperti. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Cibo regale, ve lo garantisco. |
| TERZO SIGNORE | Non [[concepts/doubt\|dubitatene]], se il denaro e la stagione possono darlo. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Come state? Che notizie ci sono? |
| TERZO SIGNORE | [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiade]] è bandito. L’avete saputo? |
| PRIMO | e SECONDO SIGNORE [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiade]] bandito? |
| TERZO SIGNORE | È così, statene certi. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Come, come? |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Di grazia, per che cosa? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Miei degni amici, volete accostarvi? |
| TERZO SIGNORE | Ve ne dirò di più fra poco. Ecco un nobile banchetto in arrivo. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | È sempre l’uomo di prima. |
| TERZO SIGNORE | Durerà, durerà? |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Dura; ma il [[motifs/time\|tempo]] farà—e così— |
| TERZO SIGNORE | Intendo. 64 |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Ognuno al suo sgabello con lo stesso slancio con cui andrebbe alle labbra della sua amante. Il vostro cibo sarà uguale in ogni posto. Non ne fate un banchetto di [[motifs/city\|città]], da lasciar raffreddare le vivande prima d’accordarci sul primo posto. Sedete, sedete. Gli dèi esigono i nostri ringraziamenti.<br>Siedono<br>Voi grandi benefattori, spargete di gratitudine la nostra compagnia. Per i vostri doni fatevi lodare; ma serbate sempre da dare, perché le vostre divinità non siano disprezzate. Prestate a ciascuno abbastanza perché l’uno non debba prestare all’altro; ché se le vostre divinità dovessero chiedere in prestito agli uomini, gli uomini abbandonerebbero gli dèi. Fate che le vivande siano amate più di chi le dona. Nessuna adunanza di venti sia senza una ventina di furfanti. Se dodici donne siedono a tavola, che una dozzina di loro sia quel che è. Il resto dei vostri nemici, o dèi—i senatori d’Atene, insieme alla comune marmaglia del popolo—ciò che in loro è guasto, voi dèi, rendetelo adatto alla distruzione. Quanto a questi miei amici presenti, come essi non sono nulla per me, così in nulla benediteli; e a nulla siano i benvenuti.—Scoprite, [[dogs\|cani]], e lappate. |
| *(didascalia)* | I piatti vengono scoperti, e si vedono pieni d’[[motifs/water\|acqua]] fumante e di pietre |
| ALCUNI SIGNORI | Che intende sua signoria? |
| ALTRI SIGNORI | Non so. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Possiate non vedere mai un banchetto migliore,<br>voi manipolo d’amici di bocca. Fumo e [[motifs/water\|acqua]] tiepida<br>è la vostra perfezione. Questo è l’ultimo di [[characters/timon\|Timone]],<br>il quale, incrostato e infiorato della vostra adulazione,<br>se la lava via, e vi spruzza in faccia<br>la vostra fetida infamia. |
| *(didascalia)* | Getta loro l’[[motifs/water\|acqua]] in faccia |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Vivete odiati e a lungo,<br>sorridentissimi, lisci, detestati parassiti,<br>cortesi distruttori, affabili lupi, miti orsi,<br>voi sciocchi della fortuna, amici da tavola, mosche del [[motifs/time\|tempo]],<br>schiavi da berretto e ginocchio, vapori, pupazzi da orologio!<br>D’uomo e di bestia l’infinito morbo<br>vi ricopra tutti d’una crosta. |
| *(didascalia)* | Un Signore se ne va |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Come, te ne vai?<br>Piano, prendi prima la tua medicina. Anche tu, e tu. |
| *(didascalia)* | Li percuote |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Fermo, ti presterò denaro, non ne prenderò a prestito. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono i Signori, lasciando berretti e toghe |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Come, tutti in fuga? D’ora innanzi non vi sia banchetto<br>in cui un [[archetypes/villain\|furfante]] non sia ospite gradito.<br>Brucia, [[house\|casa]]! Sprofonda, Atene! D’ora innanzi siano odiati<br>da [[characters/timon\|Timone]] l’uomo e tutta l’umanità! |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano i Senatori e gli altri Signori |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Ebbene, miei signori? |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Conoscete la qualità del furore del signor [[characters/timon\|Timone]]? |
| TERZO SIGNORE | Puah! Avete visto il mio berretto? |
| QUARTO SIGNORE | Ho perso la mia toga. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Non è che un signore pazzo, e nulla se non gli umori lo governa. Mi diede un [[motifs/jewel\|gioiello]] l’altro giorno, e ora me l’ha battuto fuori dal cappello. Avete visto il mio [[motifs/jewel\|gioiello]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | TERZO SIGNORE Avete visto il mio berretto? |
| *(didascalia)* | SECONDO SIGNORE |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Eccolo qui. |
| QUARTO SIGNORE | Qui giace la mia toga. |
| PRIMO SIGNORE | Non indugiamo. |
| SECONDO SIGNORE | Il signor [[characters/timon\|Timone]] è pazzo. |
| TERZO SIGNORE | Lo sento sulle mie ossa. |
| QUARTO SIGNORE | Un giorno ci dà diamanti, il giorno dopo pietre. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_1" data-title="Timon of Athens — Act IV, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="motif/grave,motif/nakedness,motif/night,motif/poison,motif/tears,concept/fear,concept/friendship,concept/truth,character/timon"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/timon\|Timon]] |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Let me look back upon thee. O thou wall<br>That girdles in those wolves, dive in the earth,<br>And fence not Athens! Matrons, turn incontinent!<br>Obedience fail in children! Slaves and fools,<br>Pluck the [[motifs/grave\|grave]] wrinkled senate from the bench<br>And minister in their steads! To general filths<br>[[convert\|Convert]] o‘th’ instant, green virginity!<br>Do’t in your parents’ eyes. Bankrupts, hold fast!<br>Rather than render back, out with your knives,<br>And cut your trusters’ throats. Bound servants, steal!<br>Large-handed robbers your [[motifs/grave\|grave]] masters are,<br>And pill by law. Maid, to thy master’s bed!<br>Thy mistress is o’th’ brothel. Son of sixteen,<br>Pluck the lined crutch from thy old limping sire;<br>With it beat out his brains! Piety and [[concepts/fear\|fear]],<br>Religion to the gods, peace, justice, [[concepts/truth\|truth]],<br>Domestic awe, [[motifs/night\|night]] rest, and neighbourhood,<br>Instruction, manners, mysteries, and trades,<br>Degrees, observances, customs, and laws,<br>Decline to your confounding contraries,<br>And let confusion live! Plagues incident to men,<br>Your potent and infectious fevers heap<br>On Athens, ripe for stroke! Thou cold sciatica,<br>Cripple our senators, that their limbs may halt<br>As lamely as their manners! Lust and liberty,<br>Creep in the minds and marrows of our youth,<br>That ‘gainst the stream of virtue they may strive<br>And drown themselves in riot! Itches, blains,<br>Sow all th’Athenian bosoms, and their crop<br>Be general leprosy! Breath infect breath,<br>That their society, as their [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]], may<br>Be merely [[motifs/poison\|poison]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | He [[motifs/tears\|tears]] off his clothes |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Nothing I’ll bear from thee<br>But [[motifs/nakedness\|nakedness]], thou detestable town;<br>Take thou that too, with multiplying bans.<br>[[characters/timon\|Timon]] will to the woods, where he shall find<br>Th‘unkindest beast more kinder than mankind.<br>The gods confound—hear me you good gods all—<br>Th’Athenians, both within and out that wall;<br>And grant, as [[characters/timon\|Timon]] grows, his hate may grow<br>To the whole race of mankind, high and low.<br>Amen. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/timon\|Timone]] |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Lascia ch’io mi volga a guardarti. O tu, muro<br>che cingi quei lupi, sprofonda nella terra,<br>e non difendere Atene! Matrone, fatevi incontinenti!<br>Manchi l’obbedienza nei figli! Schiavi e sciocchi,<br>strappate dal seggio il [[motifs/grave\|grave]] senato rugoso<br>e governate in loro vece! In lordure comuni<br>[[convert\|convertitevi]] all’istante, verde verginità!<br>Fatelo sotto gli occhi dei vostri genitori. Falliti, tenete duro!<br>Piuttosto che restituire, fuori i coltelli,<br>e tagliate la gola a chi vi diede fiducia. Servi legati, rubate!<br>Ladri dalla mano larga sono i vostri [[motifs/grave\|gravi]] padroni,<br>e saccheggiano per legge. Ancella, al letto del tuo padrone!<br>La tua padrona è al bordello. Figlio di sedici anni,<br>strappa la gruccia imbottita al tuo vecchio padre zoppo;<br>e con quella fagli saltare le cervella! Pietà e [[concepts/fear\|timore]],<br>religione verso gli dèi, pace, giustizia, [[concepts/truth\|verità]],<br>rispetto domestico, riposo della [[motifs/night\|notte]], e buon vicinato,<br>istruzione, costumi, misteri e mestieri,<br>gradi, osservanze, usanze e leggi,<br>degenerate nei vostri contrari che tutto confondono,<br>e viva la confusione! Pesti che toccate agli uomini,<br>ammucchiate le vostre potenti e infettive febbri<br>su Atene, matura per il colpo! Tu, fredda sciatica,<br>storpia i nostri senatori, sì che le loro membra zoppichino<br>come zoppicano i loro costumi! Lussuria e licenza,<br>insinuatevi nelle menti e nei midolli della nostra gioventù,<br>sì che contro la corrente della virtù essa si sforzi<br>e s’anneghi nella dissolutezza! Pruriti e pustole,<br>seminate tutti i petti ateniesi, e il loro raccolto<br>sia lebbra universale! Il fiato infetti il fiato,<br>sì che il loro consorzio, come la loro [[concepts/friendship\|amicizia]], sia<br>puro e semplice [[motifs/poison\|veleno]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | Si [[motifs/tears\|strappa]] di dosso le vesti |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Nulla porterò via da te<br>se non la [[motifs/nakedness\|nudità]], città detestabile;<br>prenditi anche quella, con moltiplicate maledizioni.<br>[[characters/timon\|Timone]] andrà ai boschi, dove troverà<br>la belva più crudele più benigna del genere umano.<br>Gli dèi confondano—uditemi, voi tutti buoni dèi—<br>gli Ateniesi, tanto dentro quanto fuori di quel muro;<br>e concedano che, come [[characters/timon\|Timone]] cresce, cresca il suo odio<br>verso tutta la razza umana, alti e bassi.<br>Amen. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_2" data-title="Timon of Athens — Act IV, Scene 2" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="scene" data-tags="motif/blood,motif/grave,concept/friendship,concept/poverty,concept/self,character/flavius,character/timon"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/flavius\|Flavius]], with two or three Servants |
| FIRST SERVANT | Hear you, master steward, where’s our master?<br>Are we undone, cast off, nothing remaining? |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | Alack, my fellows, what should I say to you?<br>Let me be recorded: by the righteous gods,<br>I am as poor as you. |
| FIRST SERVANT | Such a [[house]] broke,<br>So noble a master fall’n? All gone, and not<br>One friend to take his fortune by the arm<br>And go along with him? |
| SECOND SERVANT | As we do turn our backs<br>From our companion thrown into his [[motifs/grave\|grave]],<br>So his familiars to his buried fortunes<br>Slink all away, leave their false vows with him<br>Like empty purses picked; and his poor [[concepts/self\|self]],<br>A dedicated beggar to the air,<br>With his disease of all-shunned [[concepts/poverty\|poverty]],<br>Walks like contempt [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter other Servants |
| SECOND SERVANT | More of our fellows. |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | All broken implements of a ruined [[house]]. |
| THIRD SERVANT | Yet do our hearts wear [[characters/timon\|Timon]]’s livery.<br>That see I by our faces. We are fellows still,<br>Serving alike in sorrow. Leaked is our barque,<br>And we, poor mates, stand on the dying deck<br>Hearing the surges’ threat. We must all part<br>Into this sea of air. |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | Good fellows all,<br>The latest of my wealth I’ll share amongst you.<br>Wherever we shall meet, for [[characters/timon\|Timon]]’s sake<br>Let’s yet be fellows. Let’s shake our heads and say,<br>As ‘twere a knell unto our master’s fortunes,<br>’We have seen better days.’<br>He gives them money<br>Let each take some.<br>Nay, put out all your hands. Not one word more.<br>Thus part we rich in sorrow, parting poor.<br>They embrace, and the Servants part several ways<br>O, the fierce wretchedness that glory brings us!<br>Who would not wish to be from wealth exempt,<br>Since riches point to misery and contempt?<br>Who would be so mocked with glory, or to live<br>But in [[A Dream\|a dream]] of [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]],<br>To have his pomp and all what state compounds<br>But only painted like his varnished friends?<br>Poor honest lord, brought low by his own heart,<br>Undone by goodness! Strange, unusual [[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>When man’s worst sin is he does too much good!<br>Who then dares to be half so kind again?<br>For bounty, that makes gods, does still mar men.<br>My dearest lord, blessed to be most accursed,<br>Rich only to be wretched, thy great fortunes<br>Are made thy chief afflictions. Alas, kind lord!<br>He’s flung in rage from this ingrateful seat<br>Of monstrous friends;<br>Nor has he with him to supply his life,<br>Or that which can command it.<br>I’ll follow and enquire him out.<br>I’ll ever serve his mind with my best will.<br>Whilst I have gold I’ll be his steward still. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/flavius\|Flavio]], con due o tre Servi |
| PRIMO SERVO | Udite, messer intendente, dov’è il nostro padrone?<br>Siamo rovinati, licenziati, non resta più nulla? |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIO]] | Ahimè, compagni miei, che dovrei dirvi?<br>Sia messo agli atti: per gli dèi giusti,<br>io sono povero quanto voi. |
| PRIMO SERVO | Una tal [[house\|casa]] rovinata,<br>un padrone così nobile caduto? Tutti spariti, e non<br>un amico che prenda per il braccio la sua fortuna<br>e vada con lui? |
| SECONDO SERVO | Come noi volgiamo le spalle<br>al compagno gettato nella sua [[motifs/grave\|tomba]],<br>così i suoi intimi, alle sue fortune sepolte,<br>svicolano via tutti, gli lasciano i loro falsi voti<br>come borse vuote e svaligiate; e il suo povero [[concepts/self\|io]],<br>mendicante votato all’aria aperta,<br>con la sua malattia della [[concepts/poverty\|povertà]] fuggita da tutti,<br>cammina [[Alone\|solo]] come il disprezzo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano altri Servi |
| SECONDO SERVO | Altri dei nostri compagni. |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIO]] | Tutti strumenti rotti d’una [[house\|casa]] in rovina. |
| TERZO SERVO | Eppure i nostri cuori portano la livrea di [[characters/timon\|Timone]].<br>Lo vedo dai nostri volti. Siamo compagni ancora,<br>servendo del pari nel dolore. La nostra barca fa acqua,<br>e noi, poveri marinai, stiamo sul ponte che muore<br>udendo la minaccia dei flutti. Dobbiamo tutti separarci<br>in questo mare d’aria. |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIO]] | Buoni compagni tutti,<br>l’ultimo della mia ricchezza lo dividerò fra voi.<br>Dovunque c’incontreremo, per amore di [[characters/timon\|Timone]]<br>siamo ancora compagni. Scuotiamo il capo e diciamo,<br>quasi fosse un rintocco per le fortune del nostro padrone,<br>‘Abbiamo visto giorni migliori.’<br>Dà loro del denaro<br>Ciascuno ne prenda un poco.<br>Su, tendete tutti le mani. Non una parola di più.<br>Così ci separiamo ricchi di dolore, separandoci poveri.<br>S’abbracciano, e i Servi si dividono per varie strade<br>Oh, la feroce miseria che la gloria ci porta!<br>Chi non vorrebbe essere esente dalla ricchezza,<br>poiché i beni conducono alla miseria e al disprezzo?<br>Chi vorrebbe essere così beffato dalla gloria, o vivere<br>solo in [[A Dream\|un sogno]] d’[[concepts/friendship\|amicizia]],<br>avere il suo fasto e tutto ciò che compone il rango<br>soltanto dipinto, come i suoi amici verniciati?<br>Povero onesto signore, abbassato dal proprio cuore,<br>rovinato dalla bontà! Strano, insolito [[motifs/blood\|sangue]]<br>quando il peggior peccato dell’uomo è fare troppo bene!<br>Chi oserà allora essere di nuovo generoso neppure a metà?<br>Ché la munificenza, che fa gli dèi, sempre rovina gli uomini.<br>Carissimo mio signore, benedetto per essere maledettissimo,<br>ricco solo per essere misero, le tue grandi fortune<br>sono divenute le tue principali afflizioni. Ahimè, buon signore!<br>S’è scagliato con furore via da questa ingrata sede<br>d’amici mostruosi;<br>né ha con sé di che sostentare la vita,<br>o ciò che possa procurarla.<br>Lo seguirò e mi metterò a cercarlo.<br>Servirò sempre il suo animo con la mia miglior volontà.<br>Finché avrò oro sarò ancora il suo intendente. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_4--scene_3" data-title="Timon of Athens — Act IV, Scene 3" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/fool,archetype/king,archetype/monster,archetype/villain,motif/birds,motif/blood,motif/cave,motif/city,motif/death,motif/decay,motif/desire,motif/fire,motif/grave,motif/horse,motif/lamb,motif/leaves,motif/lion,motif/love,motif/moon,motif/nature,motif/pain,motif/poison,motif/rose,motif/snow,motif/storm,motif/swallow,motif/tears,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,motif/window,motif/winter,concept/ambition,concept/charity,concept/doubt,concept/fear,concept/friendship,concept/honour,concept/hope,concept/melancholy,concept/mercy,concept/nature,concept/pride,concept/procreation,concept/self,concept/wonder,form/fragment,character/alcibiades,character/apemantus,character/flavius,character/hyperion,character/timon"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/timon\|Timon]] from his [[motifs/cave\|cave]] in the woods, half naked, and with a spade |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | O blessèd breeding sun, draw from the earth<br>Rotten humidity; below thy sister’s orb<br>Infect the air. Twinned brothers of one womb,<br>Whose [[concepts/procreation\|procreation]], residence, and birth<br>Scarce is dividant, touch them with several fortunes,<br>The greater scorns the lesser. Not [[concepts/nature\|nature]],<br>To whom all sores lay siege, can bear great fortune<br>But by contempt of [[concepts/nature\|nature]].<br>It is the pasture lards the brother’s sides,<br>The want that makes him lean.<br>Raise me this beggar and demit that lord,<br>The senator shall bear contempt hereditary,<br>The beggar native [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. Who dares, who dares<br>In purity of manhood stand upright<br>And say ‘This man’s a flatterer’? If one be,<br>So are they all, for every grece of fortune<br>Is smoothed by that below. The learnèd pate<br>Ducks to the golden [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. All’s obliquy;<br>There’s nothing level in our cursed natures<br>But direct villainy. Therefore be abhorred<br>All feasts, societies, and throngs of men.<br>His semblable, yea, himself, [[characters/timon\|Timon]] disdains.<br>Destruction fang mankind. Earth, yield me roots.<br>He digs<br>Who seeks for better of thee, sauce his palate<br>With thy most operant [[motifs/poison\|poison]].<br>He finds gold<br>What is here?<br>Gold? Yellow, glittering, precious gold?<br>No, gods, I am no idle votarist:<br>Roots, you clear heavens. Thus much of this will<br>make<br>Black white, foul fair, wrong right,<br>Base noble, old young, coward valiant.<br>Ha, you gods! Why this, what, this, you gods? Why,<br>this<br>Will lug your priests and servants from your sides,<br>Pluck stout men’s pillows from below their heads.<br>This yellow slave<br>Will knit and break religions, bless th’accursed,<br>Make the hoar leprosy adored, place thieves,<br>And give them title, knee, and approbation<br>With senators on the bench. This is it<br>That makes the wappered widow wed again.<br>She whom the spittle [[house]] and ulcerous sores<br>Would cast the gorge at, this embalms and spices<br>To th’ April day again. Come, damned earth,<br>Thou common whore of mankind, that puts odds<br>Among the rout of nations; I will make thee<br>Do thy right [[concepts/nature\|nature]].<br>March afar off<br>Ha, a drum! Thou’rt quick;<br>But yet I’ll bury thee.<br>He buries gold<br>Thou’lt go, strong thief,<br>When gouty keepers of thee cannot stand.<br>He keeps some gold<br>Nay, stay thou out for earnest. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiades]], with soldiers playing drum and |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | fife, in warlike manner; and Phrynia and Timandra |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | What art thou there? Speak. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | A beast, as thou art. The canker gnaw thy heart<br>For showing me again the eyes of man. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | What is thy name? Is man so hateful to thee<br>That art thyself a man? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I am Misanthropos, and hate mankind.<br>For thy part, I do wish thou wert a dog,<br>That I might [[motifs/love\|love]] thee something. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | I know thee [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>But in thy fortunes am unlearned and strange. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I know thee too, and more than that I know thee<br>I not [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to know. Follow thy drum.<br>With man’s [[motifs/blood\|blood]] paint the ground gules, gules.<br>Religious canons, civil laws, are cruel;<br>Then what should war be? This fell whore of thine<br>Hath in her more destruction than thy [[Sword\|sword]],<br>For all her cherubin look. |
| PHRYNIA | Thy lips rot off! |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I will not kiss thee; then the rot returns<br>To thine own lips again. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | How came the noble [[characters/timon\|Timon]] to this change? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | As the [[motifs/moon\|moon]] does, by wanting light to give.<br>But then renew I could not like the [[motifs/moon\|moon]];<br>There were no suns to borrow of. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Noble [[characters/timon\|Timon]], what [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] may I do thee? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | None but to maintain my opinion. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | What is it, [[characters/timon\|Timon]]? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Promise me [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]], but perform none. If thou wilt promise, the gods plague thee, for thou art a man. If thou dost not perform, confound thee, for thou art a man. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | I have heard in some sort of thy miseries. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Thou saw’st them when I had prosperity. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | I see them now; then was a blessèd [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | As thine is now, held with a brace of harlots. |
| TIMANDRA | Is this th’Athenian minion, whom the world<br>Voiced so regardfully? |
| SIMON | Art thou Timandra? |
| TIMANDRA | Yes. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Be a whore still. They [[motifs/love\|love]] thee not that use thee.<br>Give them diseases, leaving with thee their lust.<br>Make use of thy salt hours: season the slaves<br>For tubs and baths, bring down [[motifs/rose\|rose]]-cheeked youth<br>To the tub-fast and the diet. |
| TIMANDRA | Hang thee, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]! |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Pardon him, sweet Timandra, for his wits<br>Are drowned and lost in his calamities.<br>I have but little gold of late, brave [[characters/timon\|Timon]],<br>The want whereof doth daily make revolt<br>In my penurious band. I have heard and grieved<br>How cursed Athens, mindless of thy worth,<br>Forgetting thy great deeds, when neighbour states<br>But for thy [[Sword\|sword]] and fortune trod upon them— |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I prithee, beat thy drum and get thee gone. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | I am thy friend, and pity thee, dear [[characters/timon\|Timon]]. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | How dost thou pity him whom thou dost trouble?<br>I had rather be [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Why, fare thee [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>Here is some gold for thee. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Keep it. I cannot eat it. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | When I have laid proud Athens on a heap— |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Warr‘st thou ’gainst Athens? |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Ay, [[characters/timon\|Timon]], and have cause. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | The gods confound them all in thy conquest,<br>And thee after, when thou hast conquered. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Why me, [[characters/timon\|Timon]]? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | That by killing of villains<br>Thou wast born to conquer my country.<br>Put up thy gold.<br>He gives [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiades]] gold<br>Go on; here’s gold; go on.<br>Be as a planetary plague when Jove<br>Will o’er some high-viced [[motifs/city\|city]] hang his [[motifs/poison\|poison]]<br>In the sick air. Let not thy [[Sword\|sword]] skip one.<br>Pity not honoured age for his white beard;<br>He is an usurer. Strike me the counterfeit matron;<br>It is her habit only that is honest,<br>Herself’s a bawd. Let not the virgin’s cheek<br>Make soft thy trenchant [[Sword\|sword]]; for those milk paps<br>That through the [[motifs/window\|window]]-bars bore at men’s eyes<br>Are not within the leaf of pity writ;<br>But set them down horrible traitors. Spare not the<br>babe<br>Whose dimpled smiles from fools exhaust their [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]].<br>Think it a bastard whom the oracle<br>Hath doubtfully pronounced thy throat shall cut,<br>And mince it sans remorse. Swear against objects.<br>Put armour on thine ears and on thine eyes<br>Whose proof nor yells of mothers, maids, nor babes,<br>Nor sight of priests in holy vestments bleeding,<br>Shall pierce a jot. There’s gold to pay thy soldiers.<br>Make large confusion, and, thy fury spent,<br>Confounded be thyself. Speak not. Be gone. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Hast thou gold yet? I’ll take the gold thou giv’st me,<br>Not all thy counsel. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Dost thou or dost thou not, heaven’s curse upon thee! |
| PHRYNIA | and TIMANDRA<br>Give us some gold, good [[characters/timon\|Timon]]. Hast thou more? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Enough to make a whore forswear her trade,<br>And to make wholesomeness a bawd. Hold up, you<br>sluts,<br>Your aprons mountant. |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws gold into their aprons |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | You are not oathable,<br>Although I know you’ll swear, terribly swear,<br>Into strong shudders and to heavenly agues<br>Th’immortal gods that hear you. Spare your oaths;<br>I’ll trust to your conditions. Be whores still,<br>And he whose pious breath seeks to [[convert]] you,<br>Be strong in whore, allure him, burn him up.<br>Let your close [[motifs/fire\|fire]] predominate his smoke;<br>And be no turncoats. Yet may your [[motifs/pain\|pain]]-sick months<br>Be quite contrary, and thatch your poor thin roofs<br>With burdens of the dead—some that were hanged,<br>No matter. Wear them, betray with them; whore still;<br>Paint till a [[motifs/horse\|horse]] may mire upon your face.<br>A pox of wrinkles! |
| PHRYNIA | and TIMANDRA [[motifs/well\|Well]], more gold; what then?<br>Believe’t that we’ll do anything for gold. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Consumptions sow<br>In hollow bones of man, strike their sharp shins,<br>And mar men’s spurring. Crack the lawyer’s voice,<br>That he may never more false title plead<br>Nor sound his quillets shrilly. Hoar the flamen<br>That scolds against the quality of flesh<br>And not believes himself. Down with the nose,<br>Down with it flat; take the bridge quite away<br>Of him that his particular to foresee<br>Smells from the general weal. Make curled-pate<br>ruffians bald,<br>And let the unscarred braggarts of the war<br>Derive some [[motifs/pain\|pain]] from you. Plague all,<br>That your activity may defeat and quell<br>The source of all erection. There’s more gold.<br>Do you damn others, and let this damn you;<br>And ditches [[motifs/grave\|grave]] you all! |
| PHRYNIA | and TIMANDRA<br>More counsel with more money, bounteous [[characters/timon\|Timon]]. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | More whore, more mischief first; I have given you earnest. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Strike up the drum towards Athens. Farewell, [[characters/timon\|Timon]].<br>If I thrive [[motifs/well\|well]], I’ll visit thee again. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | If I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] [[motifs/well\|well]], I’ll never see thee more. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | I never did thee harm. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Yes, thou spok’st [[motifs/well\|well]] of me. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | Call’st thou that harm? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Men daily find it. Get thee away,<br>And take thy beagles with thee. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADES]] | We but offend him. Strike! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt to drum and fife all but [[characters/timon\|Timon]] |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | That [[concepts/nature\|nature]], being sick of man’s unkindness,<br>Should yet be hungry!<br>He digs the earth<br>Common mother—thou<br>Whose womb unmeasurable and infinite breast<br>Teems and feeds all, whose selfsame mettle<br>Whereof thy proud [[archetypes/child\|child]], arrogant man, is puffed<br>Engenders the black toad and adder blue,<br>The gilded newt and eyeless venomed worm,<br>With all th‘abhorrèd births below crisp heaven<br>Whereon [[characters/hyperion\|Hyperion]]’s quick’ning [[motifs/fire\|fire]] doth shine—<br>Yield him who all thy human sons do hate<br>From forth thy plenteous bosom, one poor root.<br>Ensear thy fertile and conceptions womb;<br>Let it no more bring out ingrateful man.<br>Go great with tigers, dragons, wolves, and bears;<br>Teem with new monsters whom thy upward face<br>Hath to the marbled mansion all above<br>Never presented.<br>He finds a root<br>O, a root! Dear thanks.<br>Dry up thy marrows, vines, and plough-torn leas,<br>Whereof ingrateful man with liquorish draughts<br>And morsels unctuous greases his pure mind,<br>That from it all consideration slips!— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/apemantus\|Apemantus]] |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | More man? Plague, plague! |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | I was directed hither. Men report<br>Thou dost affect my manners, and dost use them. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | ’Tis then because thou dost not keep a dog<br>Whom I would imitate. Consumption catch thee! |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | This is in thee a [[concepts/nature\|nature]] but infected,<br>A poor unmanly [[concepts/melancholy\|melancholy]], sprung<br>From change of fortune. Why this spade, this place,<br>This slave-like habit, and these looks of care?<br>Thy flatterers yet wear silk, drink wine, lie soft,<br>Hug their diseased perfumes, and have forgot<br>That ever [[characters/timon\|Timon]] was. Shame not these woods<br>By putting on the cunning of a carper.<br>Be thou a flatterer now, and seek to thrive<br>By that which has undone thee. Hinge thy knee,<br>And let his very breath whom thou‘lt observe<br>Blow off thy cap. Praise his most vicious strain,<br>And call it excellent. Thou wast told thus.<br>Thou gav’st thine ears like tapsters that bade welcome<br>To knaves and all approachers. ’Tis most just<br>That thou turn rascal. Hadst thou wealth again,<br>Rascals should have’t. Do not assume my likeness. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Were I like thee, I’d throw away myself. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Thou hast cast away thyself being like thyself—<br>A madman so long, now a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. What, think‘st<br>That the bleak air, thy boisterous chamberlain,<br>Will put thy shirt on warm? Will these mossed trees<br>That have outlived the eagle page thy heels<br>And skip when thou point’st out? Will the cold brook,<br>Candied with ice, caudle thy morning taste<br>To cure thy o’ernight’s surfeit? Call the creatures<br>Whose naked natures live in all the spite<br>Of wreakful heaven, whose bare unhousèd trunks<br>To the conflicting elements exposed<br>Answer mere [[concepts/nature\|nature]]; bid them flatter thee.<br>O, thou shalt find— |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | A [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] of thee! Depart. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | I [[motifs/love\|love]] thee better now than e’er I did. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I hate thee worse. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Why? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Thou flatter’st misery. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | I flatter not, but say thou art a caitiff. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Why dost thou seek me out? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | To vex thee. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Always a [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]’s office, or a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]’s.<br>Dost please thyself in’t? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Ay. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | What, a knave too? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | If thou didst put this sour cold habit on<br>To castigate thy [[concepts/pride\|pride]], ‘twere [[motifs/well\|well]]; but thou<br>Dost it enforcèdly. Thou’dst courtier be again<br>Wert thou not beggar. Willing misery<br>Outlives incertain pomp, is crowned before.<br>The one is filling still, never complete;<br>The other at high wish. Best state, contentless,<br>Hath a distracted and most wretched being,<br>Worse than the worst, content.<br>Thou shouldst [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to die, being miserable. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Not by his breath that is more miserable.<br>Thou art a slave whom fortune’s tender arm<br>With favour never clasped, but bred a dog.<br>Hadst thou like us from our first swathe proceeded<br>The sweet degrees that this brief world affords<br>To such as may the passive drudges of it<br>Freely command, thou wouldst have plunged thyself<br>In general riot, melted down thy youth<br>In different beds of lust, and never learned<br>The icy precepts of respect, but followed<br>The sugared game before thee. But myself,<br>Who had the world as my confectionary,<br>The mouths, the tongues, the eyes and hearts of men<br>At duty, more than I could frame employment,<br>That numberless upon me stuck, as [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]]<br>Do on the oak, have with one [[motifs/winter\|winter]]’s brush<br>Fell from their boughs, and left me open, bare<br>For every [[motifs/storm\|storm]] that blows—I to bear this,<br>That never knew but better, is some burden.<br>Thy [[concepts/nature\|nature]] did commence in sufferance, [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Hath made thee hard in’t. Why shouldst thou hate men?<br>They never flattered thee. What hast thou given?<br>If thou wilt curse, thy father, that poor rag,<br>Must be thy subject, who in spite put stuff<br>To some she-beggar and compounded thee<br>Poor rogue hereditary. Hence, be gone.<br>If thou hadst not been born the worst of men<br>Thou hadst been a knave and flatterer. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Art thou proud yet? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Ay, that I am not thee. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | I that I was<br>No prodigal. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I that I am one now.<br>Were all the wealth I have shut up in thee<br>I’d give thee leave to hang it. Get thee gone.<br>That the whole life of Athens were in this!<br>Thus would I eat it.<br>He bites the root |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] offering food Here, I will mend thy feast. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | First mend my company: take away thyself. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | So I shall mend mine own by th’ lack of thine. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | ’Tis not [[motifs/well\|well]] mended so, it is but botched;<br>If not, I would it were. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | What wouldst thou have to Athens? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Thee thither in a whirlwind. If thou wilt,<br>Tell them there I have gold. Look, so I have. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Here is no use for gold. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | The best and truest,<br>For here it sleeps and does no hired harm. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Where liest a-nights, [[characters/timon\|Timon]]? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Under that’s above me. Where feed’st thou a-days, [[characters/apemantus\|Apemantus]]? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Where my stomach finds meat; or rather, where I eat it. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Would [[motifs/poison\|poison]] were obedient, and knew my mind! |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Where wouldst thou send it? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | To sauce thy dishes. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | The middle of humanity thou never knewest, but the extremity of both ends. When thou wast in thy gilt and thy perfume, they mocked thee for too much curiosity; in thy rags thou know’st none, but art despised for the contrary. There’s a medlar for thee; eat it. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | On what I hate I feed not. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Dost hate a medlar? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Ay, though it look like thee. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | An thou’dst hated meddlers sooner, thou shouldst have loved thyself better now. What man didst thou ever know unthrift that was beloved after his means? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Who, without those means thou talk’st of, didst thou ever know beloved? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Myself. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I understand thee: thou hadst some means to keep a dog. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | What things in the world canst thou nearest compare to thy flatterers? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Women nearest; but men, men are the things themselves. What wouldst thou do with the world, [[characters/apemantus\|Apemantus]], if it lay in thy power? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Give it the beasts, to be rid of the men. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Wouldst thou have thyself fall in the confusion of men, and remain a beast with the beasts? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Ay, [[characters/timon\|Timon]]. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | A beastly [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]], which the gods grant thee t‘attain to. If thou wert the [[motifs/lion\|lion]], the fox would beguile thee. If thou wert the [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]], the fox would eat thee. If thou wert the fox, the [[motifs/lion\|lion]] would suspect thee when peradventure thou wert accused by the ass. If thou wert the ass, thy dullness would torment thee, and still thou lived’st but as a breakfast to the wolf. If thou wert the wolf, thy greediness would afflict thee, and oft thou shouldst hazard thy life for thy dinner. Wert thou the unicorn, [[concepts/pride\|pride]] and wrath would confound thee, and make thine own [[concepts/self\|self]] the conquest of thy fury. Wert thou a bear, thou wouldst be killed by the [[motifs/horse\|horse]]. Wert thou a [[motifs/horse\|horse]], thou wouldst be seized by the leopard. Wert thou a leopard, thou wert german to the [[motifs/lion\|lion]], and the spots of thy kindred were jurors on thy life; all thy safety were remotion, and thy defence absence. What beast couldst thou be that were not subject to a beast? And what a beast art thou already, that seest not thy loss in transformation! |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | If thou couldst please me with speaking to me, thou mightst have hit upon it here. The commonwealth of Athens is become a forest of beasts. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | How, has the ass broke the wall, that thou art out of the [[motifs/city\|city]]? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Yonder comes a poet and a painter. The plague of company light upon thee! I will [[concepts/fear\|fear]] to catch it, and give way. When I know not what else to do, I’ll see thee again. 356 |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | When there is nothing living but thee, thou shalt be welcome. I had rather be a beggar’s dog than [[characters/apemantus\|Apemantus]]. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Thou art the cap of all the fools alive. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Would thou wert clean enough to spit upon. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | A plague on thee! Thou art too bad to curse. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | All villains that do stand by thee are pure. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | There is no leprosy but what thou speak’st. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | If I name thee.<br>I’d beat thee, but I should infect my hands. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | I would my tongue could rot them off. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Away, thou issue of a mangy dog!<br>Choler does kill me that thou art alive.<br>I swoon to see thee. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Would thou wouldst burst! |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Away, thou tedious rogue! |
| *(didascalia)* | He throws a stone at [[characters/apemantus\|Apemantus]] |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | I am sorry I shall lose a stone by thee. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Beast! |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Slave! |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Toad! |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Rogue, rogue, rogue!<br>I am sick of this false world, and will [[motifs/love\|love]] naught<br>But even the mere necessities upon’t.<br>Then, [[characters/timon\|Timon]], presently prepare thy [[motifs/grave\|grave]].<br>Lie where the light foam of the sea may beat<br>Thy gravestone daily. Make thine epitaph,<br>That [[motifs/death\|death]] in me at others’ lives may laugh.<br>He looks on the gold<br>O, thou sweet [[archetypes/king\|king]]-killer, and dear [[divorce]]<br>‘Twixt natural son and sire; thou bright defiler<br>Of Hymen’s purest bed; thou valiant Mars;<br>Thou ever young, fresh, loved, and delicate wooer,<br>Whose blush doth thaw the consecrated [[motifs/snow\|snow]]<br>That lies on Dian’s lap; thou visible god,<br>That sold’rest close impossibilities<br>And mak‘st them kiss, that speak’st with every tongue<br>To every purpose; O thou touch of hearts:<br>Think thy slave man rebels, and by thy virtue<br>Set them into confounding odds, that beasts<br>May have the world in empire. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Would ’twere so,<br>But not till I am dead. I’ll say thou’st gold.<br>Thou wilt be thronged to shortly. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Thronged to? |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Ay. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Thy back, I prithee. |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | Live, and [[motifs/love\|love]] thy misery. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Long live so, and so die. I am quit. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Banditti, thieves |
| [[characters/apemantus\|APEMANTUS]] | More things like men. Eat, [[characters/timon\|Timon]], and abhor them. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| FIRST THIEF | Where should he have this gold? It is some poor [[forms/fragment\|fragment]], some slender ort of his remainder. The mere want of gold and the falling-from of his friends drove him into this [[concepts/melancholy\|melancholy]]. |
| SECOND THIEF | It is noised he hath a mass of treasure. |
| THIRD THIEF | Let us make the assay upon him. If he care not for’t, he will supply us easily. If he covetously reserve it, how shall ’s get it? |
| SECOND THIEF | True, for he bears it not about him; ’tis hid. |
| FIRST THIEF | Is not this he? |
| OTHER THIEVES | Where? |
| SECOND THIEF | ’Tis his description. |
| THIRD THIEF | He, I know him. |
| ALL THIEVES | (coming forward) Save thee, [[characters/timon\|Timon]]. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Now, thieves. |
| ALL THIEVES | Soldiers, not thieves. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Both, too, and women’s sons. |
| ALL THIEVES | We are not thieves, but men that much do want. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Your greatest want is, you want much of meat.<br>Why should you want? Behold, the earth hath roots. |
| *(didascalia)* | Within this mile break forth a hundred springs. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | The oaks bear mast, the briars scarlet hips.<br>The bounteous housewife [[concepts/nature\|nature]] on each bush<br>Lays her full mess before you. Want? Why want? |
| FIRST THIEF | We cannot live on grass, on berries, [[motifs/water\|water]],<br>As beasts and [[motifs/birds\|birds]] and fishes. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Nor on the beasts themselves, the [[motifs/birds\|birds]] and fishes;<br>You must eat men. Yet thanks I must you con<br>That you are thieves professed, that you work not<br>In holier shapes; for there is boundless theft<br>In limited professions. (Giving gold) Rascal thieves,<br>Here’s gold. Go suck the subtle [[motifs/blood\|blood]] o‘th’ grape<br>Till the high fever seethe your [[motifs/blood\|blood]] to froth,<br>And so scape hanging. Trust not the physician:<br>His antidotes are [[motifs/poison\|poison]], and he slays<br>More than you rob. Take wealth and lives together.<br>Do villainy; do, since you protest to do’t,<br>Like workmen. I’ll example you with thievery.<br>The sun’s a thief, and with his great attraction<br>Robs the vast sea. The [[motifs/moon\|moon]]’s an arrant thief,<br>And her pale [[motifs/fire\|fire]] she snatches from the sun.<br>The sea’s a thief, whose liquid surge resolves<br>The [[motifs/moon\|moon]] into salt [[motifs/tears\|tears]]. The earth’s a thief,<br>That feeds and breeds by a composture stol’n<br>From gen‘ral excrement. Each thing’s a thief.<br>The laws, your curb and whip, in their rough power<br>Has unchecked theft. [[motifs/love\|Love]] not yourselves. Away,<br>Rob one another. There’s more gold. Cut throats;<br>All that you meet are thieves. To Athens go,<br>Break open shops; nothing can you steal<br>But thieves do lose it. Steal no less for this I give you,<br>And gold confound you howsoe’er. Amen. |
| THIRD THIEF | He’s almost charmed me from my profession by persuading me to it. |
| FIRST THIEF | ’Tis in the malice of mankind that he thus advises us, not to have us thrive in our mystery. |
| SECOND THIEF | I’ll believe him as an enemy, and give over my trade. |
| FIRST THIEF | Let us first see peace in Athens. There is no [[motifs/time\|time]] so miserable but a man may be true. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Thieves |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/flavius\|Flavius]] to [[characters/timon\|Timon]] |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | O you gods!<br>Is yon despised and ruinous man my lord,<br>Full of [[motifs/decay\|decay]] and failing? O monument<br>And [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] of good deeds evilly bestowed!<br>What an alteration of [[concepts/honour\|honour]] has desp’rate want made!<br>What viler thing upon the earth than friends,<br>Who can bring noblest minds to basest ends!<br>How rarely does it meet with this [[motifs/time\|time]]’s guise,<br>When man was wished to [[motifs/love\|love]] his enemies!<br>Grant I may ever [[motifs/love\|love]] and rather woo<br>Those that would mischief me than those that do!<br>[[characters/timon\|Timon]] sees him<br>He’s caught me in his eye. I will present<br>My honest grief unto him, and as my lord<br>Still serve him with my life.—My dearest master. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Away! What art thou? |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | Have you forgot me, sir? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Why dost ask that? I have forgot all men;<br>Then if thou grant‘st thou’rt man, I have forgot thee. |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | An honest poor servant of yours. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Then I know thee not. I never had<br>Honest man about me; ay, all I kept were knaves,<br>To serve in meat to villains. |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | The gods are witness,<br>Ne’er did poor steward wear a truer grief<br>For his undone lord than mine eyes for you. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | What, dost thou weep? Come. nearer then; I [[motifs/love\|love]] thee<br>Because thou art a woman, and disclaim’st<br>Flinty mankind whose eyes do never give<br>But thorough lust and laughter. Pity’s sleeping.<br>Strange times, that weep with laughing, not with<br>weeping! |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | I beg of you to know me, good my lord,<br>T’accept my grief, |
| *(didascalia)* | He offers his money |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | and whilst this poor wealth lasts<br>To entertain me as your steward still. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Had I a steward<br>So true, so just, and now so comfortable?<br>It almost turns my dangerous [[concepts/nature\|nature]] mild.<br>Let me behold thy face. Surely this man<br>Was born of woman.<br>Forgive my general and exceptless rashness,<br>You perpetual sober gods! I do proclaim<br>One honest man—mistake me not, but one,<br>No more, I pray—and he’s a steward.<br>How fain would I have hated all mankind,<br>And thou redeem‘st thyself! But all save thee<br>I fell with curses.<br>Methinks thou art more honest now than wise,<br>For by oppressing and betraying me<br>Thou mightst have sooner got another service;<br>For many so arrive at second masters<br>Upon their first lord’s neck. But tell me true—<br>For I must ever [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]], though ne’er so sure—<br>Is not thy kindness subtle, covetous,<br>A usuring kindness, and, as rich men deal gifts,<br>Expecting in return twenty for one? |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | No, my most worthy master, in whose breast<br>[[concepts/doubt\|Doubt]] and suspect, alas, are placed too late.<br>You should have feared false times when you did feast.<br>Suspect still comes where an estate is least.<br>That which I show, heaven knows, is merely [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>Duty and zeal to your unmatched mind,<br>Care of your food and living; and, believe it,<br>My most honoured lord,<br>For any benefit that points to me,<br>Either in [[concepts/hope\|hope]] or present, I’d exchange<br>For this one wish: that you had power and wealth<br>To requite me by making rich yourself. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Look thee, ’tis so. Thou singly honest man, |
| *(didascalia)* | He gives [[characters/flavius\|Flavius]] gold |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | Here, take. The gods, out of my misery,<br>Has sent thee treasure. Go, live rich and happy,<br>But thus conditioned: thou shalt build from men,<br>Hate all, curse all, show [[concepts/charity\|charity]] to none,<br>But let the famished flesh slide from the bone<br>Ere thou relieve the beggar. Give to [[dogs]]<br>What thou deniest to men. Let prisons [[motifs/swallow\|swallow]] ‘em,<br>Debts wither ’em to nothing; be men like blasted woods,<br>And may diseases lick up their false bloods.<br>And so farewell, and thrive. |
| [[characters/flavius\|FLAVIUS]] | O, let me stay<br>And comfort you, my master. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMON]] | If thou hat’st curses,<br>Stay not. Fly whilst thou art blest and free.<br>Ne’er see thou man, and let me ne’er see thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/timon\|Timon]] into his [[motifs/cave\|cave]], [[characters/flavius\|Flavius]] another way |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/timon\|Timone]] dalla sua [[motifs/cave\|caverna]] nel bosco, seminudo, e con una vanga |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | O benedetto sole generatore, trai dalla terra<br>umidità putrida; sotto l’orbe di tua sorella<br>infetta l’aria. Fratelli gemelli d’un solo ventre,<br>la cui [[concepts/procreation\|procreazione]], dimora e nascita<br>a stento si distinguono: toccali con fortune diverse,<br>e il maggiore disprezza il minore. Non la [[concepts/nature\|natura]],<br>cui ogni piaga assedia, può sopportare gran fortuna<br>se non col disprezzo della [[concepts/nature\|natura]].<br>È il pascolo che ingrassa i fianchi del fratello,<br>la penuria che lo fa magro.<br>Innalzami questo mendicante e abbassa quel signore,<br>e il senatore porterà un disprezzo ereditario,<br>il mendicante un [[concepts/honour\|onore]] nativo. Chi osa, chi osa<br>in purezza di virilità star ritto<br>e dire ‘Costui è un adulatore’? Se uno lo è,<br>lo sono tutti, ché ogni gradino della fortuna<br>è spianato da quello di sotto. La testa dotta<br>s’inchina allo [[archetypes/fool\|sciocco]] dorato. Tutto è obliquo;<br>nulla è diritto nelle nostre nature maledette<br>se non la schietta scelleratezza. Siano dunque aborriti<br>tutti i banchetti, i consorzi e le folle d’uomini.<br>Il suo simile, anzi sé stesso, [[characters/timon\|Timone]] disdegna.<br>La distruzione azzanni il genere umano. Terra, dammi radici.<br>Scava<br>Chi da te cerca di meglio, si condisca il palato<br>col tuo più efficace [[motifs/poison\|veleno]].<br>Trova dell’oro<br>Che c’è qui?<br>Oro? Giallo, scintillante, prezioso oro?<br>No, dèi, non sono un vano supplicante:<br>radici, o cieli limpidi. Tanto così di questo può<br>fare<br>il nero bianco, il turpe bello, il torto ragione,<br>il vile nobile, il vecchio giovane, il codardo valoroso.<br>Ah, voi dèi! Perché questo, come, questo, voi dèi? Ecco,<br>questo<br>vi strapperà i sacerdoti e i servi dai fianchi,<br>toglierà i guanciali di sotto il capo d’uomini robusti.<br>Questo schiavo giallo<br>annoderà e spezzerà religioni, benedirà i maledetti,<br>farà adorare la lebbra canuta, collocherà i ladri,<br>e darà loro titolo, riverenza e approvazione<br>fra i senatori in seggio. È questo<br>che fa rimaritare la vedova sfiorita.<br>Colei di cui la [[house\|casa]] dei lebbrosi e le piaghe ulcerose<br>farebbero rivoltar lo stomaco, questo l’imbalsama e la profuma<br>di nuovo in giorno d’aprile. Vieni, dannata terra,<br>tu comune meretrice del genere umano, che semini discordia<br>fra la calca delle nazioni; io ti farò<br>compiere la tua vera [[concepts/nature\|natura]].<br>Marcia in lontananza<br>Ah, un tamburo! Sei viva;<br>ma pure ti seppellirò.<br>Seppellisce l’oro<br>Andrai, ladro potente,<br>quando i tuoi gottosi custodi non potranno reggersi in piedi.<br>Tiene un po’ d’oro<br>No, resta tu fuori come caparra. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[characters/alcibiades\|Alcibiade]], con soldati che suonano tamburo e |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | piffero, in assetto di guerra; e Frinia e Timandra |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Chi sei tu costì? Parla. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Una bestia, come sei tu. Il verme ti roda il cuore<br>per avermi mostrato di nuovo gli occhi d’un uomo. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Qual è il tuo nome? È l’uomo tanto odioso a te<br>che pure sei un uomo? |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Sono Misantropo, e odio il genere umano.<br>Per parte tua, vorrei che tu fossi un cane,<br>ch’io potessi [[motifs/love\|amarti]] un poco. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Ti conosco [[motifs/well\|bene]],<br>ma delle tue fortune sono ignaro ed estraneo. |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Anch’io conosco te, e più di quanto ti conosco<br>non [[motifs/desire\|desidero]] conoscere. Segui il tuo tamburo.<br>Col [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] degli uomini dipingi il suolo di rosso, di rosso.<br>I canoni religiosi, le leggi civili, sono crudeli;<br>che dovrebbe essere allora la guerra? Questa tua feroce sgualdrina<br>ha in sé più distruzione della tua [[Sword\|spada]],<br>malgrado tutto il suo viso di cherubino. |
| FRINIA | Ti caschino le labbra imputridite! |
| [[characters/timon\|TIMONE]] | Non ti bacerò; così il marciume ritorna<br>di nuovo sulle tue stesse labbra. |
| [[characters/alcibiades\|ALCIBIADE]] | Come giunse il nobile [[characters/timon\|Timone]] a questo mutamento? |
